<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We determine the sharp logarithmic exponent of the off-diagonal Ramsey number $`r(5,t)`$:
> 
> $`\displaystyle r(5,t)=\frac{t^4}{(\log t)^{3+o(1)}} \qquad (t\longrightarrow\infty).`$

- **C01** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): There is an absolute constant $C>0$ such that, for every $\varepsilon>0$, all sufficiently large integers $t$ satisfy $$\frac{t^4}{(\log t)^{3+\varepsilon}} \ \le\ r(5,t)\ \le\ C\frac{t^4}{(\log t)^3}.$$ The threshold for $t$ may depend on $\varepsilon$. Consequently, $$\lim_{t\to\infty} \frac{4\log t-\log r(5,t)}{\log\log t}=3.$$
  - Explanation: The upper and lower bounds determine the logarithmic exponent while allowing a threshold depending on the requested slack.
- **C02** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-upper)): There is an absolute constant $C$ such that, for every integer $t\ge2$, $$r(5,t)\le C\frac{t^4}{(\log t)^3}.$$
  - Explanation: The upper estimate holds with an absolute constant across the stated integer range.
- **C03** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-occupancy)): Let $H$ be a finite triangle-free graph on $n$ vertices, and let $D\ge1$ be a real upper bound on its maximum degree. Then $$\alpha(H)\ge\frac{n\log(D+1)}{8(D+1)}.$$
  - Explanation: Triangle-free graphs admit a logarithmic improvement over the elementary degree-based independence estimate.
- **C04** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-sample-triangle-free)): Let $G$ be a finite graph on $n$ vertices with maximum degree $d>0$. Suppose every adjacent pair has at most $m$ common neighbors, where $m>0$, $dm\ge1$, and $p=(dm)^{-1/2}$ satisfies $pd\ge1$. Then $G$ has an induced triangle-free subgraph $H$ with $$\|V(H)\|\ge\frac34pn, \qquad \Delta(H)\le12pd.$$
  - Explanation: Random sampling followed by degree and triangle deletion preserves a large induced triangle-free graph.
- **C05** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-incidence)): Let $d\ge2$, and put $p_d=Q_{d-1}/Q_d=(1-1/Q_d)/q$. For real weights $w_a$ on $\mathcal P_d$, set $W(b)=\sum_{a\perp b}w_a$ and $w=\sum_a w_a$. Then $$\frac1{Q_d}\sum_b W(b)=p_dw,\qquad \sum_b\bigl(W(b)-p_dw\bigr)^2 \le q^{d-1}\sum_a w_a^2.$$ The same assertions hold with the two spaces interchanged. In particular, for $A\subseteq\mathcal P_d$ and $B\subseteq\mathcal P_d^*$, $$\left\|e(A,B)-p_d\|A\|\|B\|\right\| \le q^{(d-1)/2}\sqrt{\|A\|\|B\|}, \qquad e(A,B)=\|\{(a,b)\in A\times B:a\perp b\}\|.$$ If $A,B\ne\varnothing$ and their incidence density is $o(1/q)$, then $\|A\|\|B\|\le Cq^{d+1}$ for all sufficiently large $q$.
  - Explanation: Projective incidence variance controls weighted deviations and yields a mixing estimate for point–hyperplane pairs.
- **C06** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-clique-free)): Every stream graph is $K_5$-free.
  - Explanation: The ordered incidence rule prevents a clique whose points would span the ambient vector space inside a hyperplane.
- **C07** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-rectangles)): With probability $1-o(1)$, the following holds simultaneously for every vector subspace $V\subseteq(\mathbb F_q^5)^*$ in the dual space: $$\#\{i:X_i\in\mathcal R(V)\}\le C\sigma,\qquad \mathcal R(V)=\{(a,b):b\subseteq V,\ a\subseteq V^\perp\}.$$ The same bound holds for the reverse-swapped stream.
  - Explanation: Uniform rectangle occupancy limits the collisions later charged in the compression argument.
- **C08** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-stream-entropy)): Suppose the stream law has density at most $C_0$, where $C_0$ is independent of $q$. By any rule, possibly using auxiliary randomness, extract a tuple $F$ of deterministic length $\ell$ in stream order or in reverse-swapped order. If $c_0k\le\ell\le C_1k$ for fixed positive constants, then $$\max_f\mathbb P(F=f)\le \frac{2C_0\binom N\ell}{M^\ell}, \qquad H(F)\ge4\sigma\ell+\eta\ell\log\sigma-O(k).$$
  - Explanation: An extracted tuple retains an entropy excess even when its selected law is biased.
- **C09** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-predictor)): Fix $0<\eta<1/10$, set $\beta=\eta/10^7$, and let $q$ be a sufficiently large prime. Write $\sigma=\log q$, and suppose $$\sigma^\beta\le D\le\sigma^{1-\eta/2},\qquad L=D\sigma^{8\beta},\qquad R\in[\sigma^\beta,2\sigma^\beta+2]\cap2\mathbb N,\qquad P=LR.$$ Let $2\le d\le4$, and let $S\subseteq U_S\subseteq\mathcal P_d$ and $T\subseteq U_T\subseteq\mathcal P_d^*$ be nonempty. Assume $$\|S\|\|T\|\ge q^{d+1}e^{-b},\qquad 0\le b\le C_bD\sigma^{6\beta},\qquad \frac{e(S,T)}{\|S\|\|T\|}\le\frac{\tau}{q},\qquad 0<\tau\le\sigma^{-200\cdot2^{d-2}\beta},$$ where $C_b$ is a fixed constant. Put $d_S=\log(\|U_S\|/\|S\|)$ and $d_T=\log(\|U_T\|/\|T\|)$. Orient the pair so that $\|S\|\le\|T\|$. There is a public-table scheme for which, except with probability $O(e^{-cq})$, the encoder can send a message of length at most $$CqP(d_S+d_T+P)$$ bits describing a set $W\subseteq U_S$ such that $$\|W\|\le \|S\|e^{CP},\qquad \|W\cap S\|\ge c\|S\|.$$ The constants may depend on $\eta,C_b$, but not on the hidden sets, $D$, or $q$. The decoder uses only $U_S,U_T$, the public parameters and tables, and the message. There is no uniformity assertion for the captured subset $W\cap S$.
  - Explanation: Public random tables describe a moderately enlarged set capturing a constant fraction of a hidden sparse-pair side, without a running-time guarantee.
- **C10** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-marking)): There is a message $\Omega$, consisting of constant-alphabet masks and $O(q\sigma)$ full flags, with the following properties. Given $(\mathsf C,\Omega)$, every unspecified flag belongs to a known set of at most $C_1q^4$ flags, where $C_1$ is an absolute constant. Moreover, $$\label{eq:marking-cost} H(\Omega\mid\mathsf C) \le O(k)+(4\sigma+\Delta+O(1))\mathbb Eh,$$ where $h\le Cq\sigma$ is the number of specified flags. Put $\theta_0=(\mathsf C,\Omega)$ and $J_0=\log(C_1q^4)$, and let $F_{\rm cheap}$ be the tuple of unspecified flags. Its expected joint entropy deficit satisfies $$\label{eq:deficit-budget} \mathbb E\bigl[(\ell-h)J_0-H_{\theta_0}(F_{\rm cheap})\bigr] \le \mathcal B:=C(\Lambda+k+\Delta q\sigma).$$ The same bound holds for any subset of unspecified positions determined by $(\mathsf C,\Omega)$.
  - Explanation: Forward and reverse marking scans restrict unspecified flags and account for the entropy spent on specified flags.
- **C11** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-decoupling)): After exposing a deterministic number of rounds of older representatives, one can draw one fresh uniform unused position from each representative block, without exposing their values, with the following properties. Let $\theta$ consist of $\theta_0$ and all older indices and values. For an unexposed position $i$, write $$\delta_i=J_0-H_\theta(F_i),\qquad I_{ij}=I_\theta(F_i;F_j).$$ Then $$\label{eq:decoupling-bounds} \mathbb E\sum_i\delta_i\le\mathcal B,\qquad \mathbb E\sum_i\max_{j\in\mathcal J_i}I_{ij} \le\frac{C\mathcal B}{qD\sigma^{3000\beta}},$$ where $\mathcal J_i$ consists of fresh representatives outside $i$’s own representative block, and contains all representatives for a middle target. The expected sums over fresh representative targets themselves are at most $C/m$ times the corresponding all-target sums. The fresh indices do not change the conditional flag marginals.
  - Explanation: A pre-round exposure reduces correlations before the fresh representative values are revealed.
- **C12** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-low-conflict)): For $i<j$, write the two flags as $(a,b),(c,y)$ and compare their actual joint law with the independent product of their conditional marginals. In a reciprocal class, or the high class $(3,3)$, there are nonnegative numbers $c_{ij}$ such that $$\label{eq:low-conflict} \Pr_{\mathrm{prod}}(a,y\text{ good},\ a\perp y) \le C(I_{ij}+c_{ij}), \qquad \sum_{i<j}c_{ij}\le C\sigma k.$$ In open reciprocal bands one can take $c_{ij}=0$.
  - Explanation: Consistency bounds independent-product incidence by mutual information and controlled rectangle collisions.
- **C13** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-high-ranks)): The retained class cannot be high. In the rank-four case, the stated assumptions instead directly produce an encoding contradicting Lemma <a href="#lem:stream-entropy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:stream-entropy">8</a>.
  - Explanation: High-rank classes force either an incidence contradiction or a description shorter than the extracted-tuple entropy allows.
- **C14** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-flat-levels)): At every good index there are independent auxiliary choices of first and second endpoint supports, each consisting of good endpoints, such that a chosen support $A$ satisfies $$\tfrac12 A_{\max}e^{-K}\le \|A\|\le A_{\max}, \qquad \mathbb E\frac{1_{\{a\in A\}}}{\|A\|}\le C p_i^A(a),$$ and similarly for $B$. For any two ordered good representatives in distinct blocks, $$\label{eq:u-monotonicity} u_j\le u_i+CK\qquad(i<j).$$
  - Explanation: Nearly uniform endpoint supports obey an approximate order relation between their endpoint scales.
- **C15** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-fresh-test)): Suppose nonempty supports $A\subseteq U_A$ and $B\subseteq U_B$ satisfy $$\|A\|\|B\|\ge q^5e^{-b},\qquad \frac{\|\{(a,b')\in A\times B:a\perp b'\}\|}{\|A\|\|B\|} \le\frac{\varepsilon}{q},$$ where $b=O(K_*)$ and $\varepsilon\le C\sigma^{-1000\beta}$. Put $n_A=\|A\|$, $n_B=\|B\|$ and $d_A=\log(\|U_A\|/n_A)$, $d_B=\log(\|U_B\|/n_B)$. Suppose a decoder-known $W$ captures at least $c n_A$ points of $A$ and has size at most $n_Ae^{CP}$.* *Using independent public proposal tables, one can describe two caps $U_A'\subseteq U_A$, $U_B'\subseteq U_B$ such that $$\label{eq:validated-caps} \|U_B'\|\le C'q^5/n_A,\quad \|U_A'\|\le C'q^5/n_B, \qquad \|B\cap U_B'\|\ge .9n_B,\quad \|A\cap U_A'\|\ge .9n_A.$$ The failure probability is $O(e^{-q})$, and the metadata cost is $O(qP(d_A+d_B+1))$. Each successful row, conditional on its past and on finding that row before its cutoff, has density bounded by an absolute constant relative to the true iid law of its stated hidden source. This conditional law does not include success of any later test or descendant.* *More generally, if such a test is produced from a hidden source contained in an original support $X$ and of size at least $c\|X\|$, then for every fixed opposite point $y$, $$\label{eq:fresh-test-domination} \mathbb E\bigl[1_{\{\text{test is produced}\}} 1_{\{y\notin\mathcal T\}}\mid\text{prior data}\bigr] \le Cq\frac{\|X\cap N(y)\|}{\|X\|}.$$ The prior data may include all original support choices and tables used earlier, but not the current fresh table.
  - Explanation: Fresh validation tests construct endpoint caps while controlling the accepted-row law before later tests or descendants are inspected.
- **C16** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-pivot-retention)): The expected number of windows lost by this construction and the preceding deletions is $o(w)$, where $w$ is the deterministic window count before deletions. Among the middle targets, the expected number lost beyond their fixed non-middle complement is $o(k)$. Consequently, with probability bounded below, a deterministic $\Theta(k)$ middle targets can be retained, in order, in the two caps of their successful pivots.
  - Explanation: Unconditional loss accounting along the tree preserves an ordered tuple of deterministic length.
- **C17** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-pivot-compression)): The output of Lemma <a href="#lem:pivot-retention" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:pivot-retention">16</a> has a new context satisfying $$\label{eq:stage-output} \Lambda'\le CqP\log(w+2)(\sigma+wP)+Cw\sigma, \qquad \Delta'\le CK_*.$$ These are deterministic bounds. After fixing public tables and conditioning on retention of the prescribed length, the stream marginal still has density bounded by a stage constant.
  - Explanation: The new context has deterministic message and domain bounds and retains bounded stream-marginal density after conditioning.
- **C18** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-compression-stage)): Suppose a selected consistent tuple and context satisfy <a href="#eq:stage-input" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:stage-input">[eq:stage-input]</a>–<a href="#eq:stage-range" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:stage-range">[eq:stage-range]</a>, with deterministic $\ell=\Theta(k)$ and bounded stream-marginal density, and suppose the rectangle-occupancy event of Lemma <a href="#lem:rectangles" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:rectangles">7</a> holds almost surely. Then either the high-rank argument contradicts Lemma <a href="#lem:stream-entropy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:stream-entropy">8</a>, or a constant-probability extraction produces another such tuple, of deterministic length $\Theta(k)$, and a new context satisfying <a href="#eq:stage-output" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:stage-output">[eq:stage-output]</a>, where $$\label{eq:window-count} w\le C\sigma^{1+\eta/2}/D.$$ Its decoder is independent of the old context.
  - Explanation: A compression stage either reaches an entropy contradiction or produces a new tuple whose decoder no longer requires the old context.
- **C19** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-stream)): For every fixed $0<\eta<1/10$ and every sufficiently large prime $q$, there is a stream of $N=\lfloor q^4\log q\rfloor$ flags whose ordered flag graph is $K_5$-free and has independence number less than $k=\lfloor q(\log q)^{1+\eta}\rfloor$.
  - Explanation: Iteration of the compression argument establishes existence of a clique-free stream graph with the stated independence threshold.
- **C20** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-training)): The construction has $n/4\le n'\le n$. If the hyperplane list or plane list is present, its length satisfies $$J_h\le n/q^2,\qquad J_p\le n/K_p=q^{1/2}\exp(-2g_0/15),$$ respectively. If both lists are present, then $J_hJ_p\le q^{13/15+o(1)}$. If a performed procedure retained a residual, that residual has the corresponding flat cap.* *If every training cell in both partitions has size less than $.04n'$, then for each query point $x$ define $O_x$ as the union of the following cells: the cell of the earliest listed hyperplane containing $x$, and the cell of the earliest listed plane containing $x$. Omit a cell when no such flat exists. Then $\|O_x\|/n'<.08$. We call this the *small-cell case*.* *The ordered lists, $n'$, all training cell sizes, and the table of intersections between the two partitions can be communicated in $O(q)$ bits. From these data the decoder determines $\|O_x\|/n'$ for every $x$, and determines which samples belong to $O_x$ from their flat memberships.
  - Explanation: Greedy flat removal retains substantial training mass and supplies a succinct description of assignment cells.
- **C21** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-rich-lines)): Suppose $n=q^{1+v}$, $v\le 3/2+O(1/\sigma)$, $n/4\le n'\le n$, $g_0>\chi_0$, and $e^{-g_0/20}<a\le2$. Put $$M=\frac{n'a}{q},\qquad K=n^{4/3}q^{-1}e^{-g_0/5}.$$ Let $X$ be a set of at most $n$ points of $\mathrm{PG}(d,q)$, where $3\le d\le4$, such that every projective plane contains at most $K$ points of $X$. The number of lines containing at least $M$ points of $X$ is at most $Cn/M$.
  - Explanation: A polynomial argument bounds rich lines when planes have bounded point concentration and the characteristic exceeds the polynomial degree.
- **C22** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-overlap-geometry)): Let $3\le d\le4$, $100qP<n=q^{1+v}\le Cq^{(d+1)/2}$, and let $S'\subset\mathrm{PG}(d,q)$ be the training set in the small-cell case of Lemma <a href="#lem:training" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:training">20</a>, with $n'=\|S'\|\in[n/4,n]$. For each query point $x$, let $O_x$ be the union of its earliest containing assignment cells in the captured hyperplane and plane lists; an absent list contributes the empty set. Put $$X_x=S'\setminus(O_x\cup\{x\}),\qquad B=\frac{q^d}{n'},\qquad a_\ell=\frac{q\|X_x\cap\ell\|}{n'}$$ for lines $\ell$ through $x$. For each $x$, let $\mathcal H_x$ be any family of hyperplanes through $x$ such that $q\|X_x\cap H\|/n'\le2$ for every $H\in\mathcal H_x$. For distinct members set $$\theta_{HH'}=\frac{q\|X_x\cap H\cap H'\|}{n'},$$ and let $E_x(a)$ count ordered distinct pairs with $\theta_{HH'}\ge a$. Let $\Delta_x(a)$ be the maximum degree of this relation.* *There is a set of $o(n)$ query points outside which, simultaneously for all dyadic $a\in[q^{-10},2]$, $$\begin{aligned} \#\{\ell\ni x:a_\ell\ge a\} &\le q^{2-2v}e^\chi a^{-100},\label{eq:local-rich-lines}\\ E_x(a)&\le B^2e^{2\chi}a^{-200},\label{eq:overlap-pairs}\\ \Delta_x(a)&\le Be^{2\chi}a^{-200}.\label{eq:overlap-degree} \end{aligned}$$ The exceptional set is independent of the choice of the families $\mathcal H_x$.* *Let $p$ be an even integer in $[10000\sigma/P,10000\sigma/P+2]$ and put $t=1/(100p)$. After excluding at most $n\exp(CP)$ additional points, $$\label{eq:ambient-overlap-degree} \Delta_x(t)\le B e^{-3P}.$$ These additional points are needed only for the ambient bound, and are not included in the $o(n)$ validation exception.* *Two further estimates hold without point exclusions. Every line strength is at most $Ce^{-g_1}$. If $g_0>\chi_0$ and $e^{-g_0/20}<a\le2$, the number of pairs $(x,\ell)$ with $x\in\ell$ and $a_\ell\ge a$ is at most $Cq^2/a^2$. For all strengths, the number of possible point–line pairs is at most $Cq^7$.
  - Explanation: The overlap estimates separate the small validation exception from the larger exception permitted only for ambient output size.
- **C24** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-score-procedure)): Assume the small-cell conclusions of Lemma <a href="#lem:training" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:training">20</a> and, when $d\ge3$, the estimates of Lemma <a href="#lem:overlap-geometry" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:overlap-geometry">22</a> for outside-own radial lines and hyperplane pairs. Assume also $$\|S\|\|T\|\ge q^{d+1}e^{-b},\qquad \frac{e(S,T)}{\|S\|\|T\|}\le\frac{\tau}{q},\qquad b=O(D\sigma^{6\beta}),$$ with the predictor’s parameter ranges and $2\le d\le4$. Take $R$ independent Poisson batches, each of mean $Lq$, sampled uniformly from $S'$. From the batches, the transmitted cell data and one additional integer $z$, this score procedure computes a set $W$ of points on the $S$-side. With probability at least $\exp[-C(P\tau+1)]$, the row has at most $2qP$ samples and satisfies $$\|W\|\le n e^{CP},\qquad \|W\cap S'\|\ge c n'.$$ These are simultaneous assertions under the original Poisson sampling law. The procedure does not condition on finding a successful row.
  - Explanation: Under the original Poisson sampling law, the score simultaneously captures training mass and bounds the ambient output size.

        "exact_quote": "$$\\max_f\\mathbb P(F=f)\\le\n \\frac{2C_0\\binom N\\ell}{M^\\ell},\n \\qquad\n H(F)\\ge4\\sigma\\ell+\\eta\\ell\\log\\sigma-O(k).$$",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-stream-entropy",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose the stream law has density at most $C_0$, where $C_0$ is independent of $q$.           "source_id": "PM-170.1"
        }

        "anchor": "stmt-thm-predictor",
        "exact_quote": "$$\\sigma^\\beta\\le D\\le\\sigma^{1-\\eta/2},\\qquad\n L=D\\sigma^{8\\beta},\\qquad\n R\\in[\\sigma^\\beta,2\\sigma^\\beta+2]\\cap2\\mathbb N,\\qquad P=LR.$$ Let $2\\le d\\le4$, and let $S\\subseteq U_S\\subseteq\\mathcal P_d$ and $T\\subseteq U_T\\subseteq\\mathcal P_d^*$ be nonempty. Assume $$|S||T|\\ge q^{d+1}e^{-b},\\qquad\n 0\\le b\\le C_bD\\sigma^{6\\beta},\\qquad\n \\frac{e(S,T)}{|S||T|}\\le\\frac{\\tau}{q},\\qquad\n 0<\\tau\\le\\sigma^{-200\\cdot2^{d-2}\\beta},$$ where $C_b$ is a fixed constant. Put $d_S=\\log(|U_S|/|S|)$ and $d_T=\\log(|U_T|/|T|)$. Orient the pair so that $|S|\\le|T|$.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-marking",
        "exact_quote": "$$\\label{eq:marking-cost}\n H(\\Omega\\mid\\mathsf C)\n \\le O(k)+(4\\sigma+\\Delta+O(1))\\mathbb Eh,$$ where $h\\le Cq\\sigma$ is the number of specified flags.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-fresh-test",
        "exact_quote": "$$|A||B|\\ge q^5e^{-b},\\qquad\n \\frac{|\\{(a,b')\\in A\\times B:a\\perp b'\\}|}{|A||B|}\n \\le\\frac{\\varepsilon}{q},$$ where $b=O(K_*)$ and $\\varepsilon\\le C\\sigma^{-1000\\beta}$. Put $n_A=|A|$, $n_B=|B|$ and $d_A=\\log(|U_A|/n_A)$, $d_B=\\log(|U_B|/n_B)$. Suppose a decoder-known $W$ captures at least $c n_A$ points of $A$ and has size at most $n_Ae^{CP}$.*\n\n*Using independent public proposal tables, one can describe two caps $U_A'\\subseteq U_A$, $U_B'\\subseteq U_B$ such that $$\\label{eq:validated-caps}\n |U_B'|\\le C'q^5/n_A,\\quad |U_A'|\\le C'q^5/n_B,\n \\qquad |B\\cap U_B'|\\ge .9n_B,\\quad |A\\cap U_A'|\\ge .9n_A.$$ The failure probability is $O(e^{-q})$, and the metadata cost is $O(qP(d_A+d_B+1))$. Each successful row, conditional on its past and on finding that row before its cutoff, has density bounded by an absolute constant relative to the true iid law of its stated hidden source. This conditional law does not include success of any later test or descendant.*\n\n\n*More generally, if such a test is produced from a hidden source contained in an original support $X$ and of size at least $c|X|$, then for every fixed opposite point $y$, $$\\label{eq:fresh-test-domination}\n \\mathbb E\\bigl[1_{\\{\\text{test is produced}\\}}\n          1_{\\{y\\notin\\mathcal T\\}}\\mid\\text{prior data}\\bigr]\n \\le Cq\\frac{|X\\cap N(y)|}{|X|}.$$ The prior data may include all original support choices and tables used earlier, but not the current fresh table.",
        "source_id": "PM-170.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-fresh-test",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose nonempty supports $A\\subseteq U_A$ and $B\\subseteq U_B$ satisfy $$|A||B|\\ge q^5e^{-b},\\qquad\n \\frac{|\\{(a,b')\\in A\\times B:a\\perp b'\\}|}{|A||B|}\n \\le\\frac{\\varepsilon}{q},$$ where $b=O(K_*)$ and $\\varepsilon\\le C\\sigma^{-1000\\beta}$. Put $n_A=|A|$, $n_B=|B|$ and $d_A=\\log(|U_A|/n_A)$, $d_B=\\log(|U_B|/n_B)$. Suppose a decoder-known $W$ captures at least $c n_A$ points of $A$ and has size at most $n_Ae^{CP}$.*\n\n*Using independent public proposal tables, one can describe two caps $U_A'\\subseteq U_A$, $U_B'\\subseteq U_B$ such that $$\\label{eq:validated-caps}\n |U_B'|\\le C'q^5/n_A,\\quad |U_A'|\\le C'q^5/n_B,\n \\qquad |B\\cap U_B'|\\ge .9n_B,\\quad |A\\cap U_A'|\\ge .9n_A.$$ The failure probability is $O(e^{-q})$, and the metadata cost is $O(qP(d_A+d_B+1))$.           "source_id": "PM-170.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Suppose nonempty supports $A\\subseteq U_A$ and $B\\subseteq U_B$ satisfy $$|A||B|\\ge q^5e^{-b},\\qquad\n \\frac{|\\{(a,b')\\in A\\times B:a\\perp b'\\}|}{|A||B|}\n \\le\\frac{\\varepsilon}{q},$$ where $b=O(K_*)$ and $\\varepsilon\\le C\\sigma^{-1000\\beta}$. Put $n_A=|A|$, $n_B=|B|$ and $d_A=\\log(|U_A|/n_A)$, $d_B=\\log(|U_B|/n_B)$. Suppose a decoder-known $W$ captures at least $c n_A$ points of $A$ and has size at most $n_Ae^{CP}$.*\n\n*Using independent public proposal tables, one can describe two caps $U_A'\\subseteq U_A$, $U_B'\\subseteq U_B$ such that $$\\label{eq:validated-caps}\n |U_B'|\\le C'q^5/n_A,\\quad |U_A'|\\le C'q^5/n_B,\n \\qquad |B\\cap U_B'|\\ge .9n_B,\\quad |A\\cap U_A'|\\ge .9n_A.$$ The failure probability is $O(e^{-q})$, and the metadata cost is $O(qP(d_A+d_B+1))$. Each successful row, conditional on its past and on finding that row before its cutoff, has density bounded by an absolute constant relative to the true iid law of its stated hidden source. This conditional law does not include success of any later test or descendant.*\n\n\n*More generally, if such a test is produced from a hidden source contained in an original support $X$ and of size at least $c|X|$, then for every fixed opposite point $y$, $$\\label{eq:fresh-test-domination}\n \\mathbb E\\bigl[1_{\\{\\text{test is produced}\\}}\n          1_{\\{y\\notin\\mathcal T\\}}\\mid\\text{prior data}\\bigr]\n \\le Cq\\frac{|X\\cap N(y)|}{|X|}.$$ The prior data may include all original support choices and tables used earlier, but not the current fresh table.",
      "explanation": "Fresh validation tests construct endpoint caps while controlling the accepted-row law before later tests or descendants are inspected.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-fresh-test",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose nonempty supports $A\\subseteq U_A$ and $B\\subseteq U_B$ satisfy $$|A||B|\\ge q^5e^{-b},\\qquad\n \\frac{|\\{(a,b')\\in A\\times B:a\\perp b'\\}|}{|A||B|}\n \\le\\frac{\\varepsilon}{q},$$ where $b=O(K_*)$ and $\\varepsilon\\le C\\sigma^{-1000\\beta}$. Put $n_A=|A|$, $n_B=|B|$ and $d_A=\\log(|U_A|/n_A)$, $d_B=\\log(|U_B|/n_B)$. Suppose a decoder-known $W$ captures at least $c n_A$ points of $A$ and has size at most $n_Ae^{CP}$.*",
          "source_id": "PM-170.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "C15",
      "kind": "conditional_result",
      "quantifiers": [],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-lem-fresh-test"
    },

        "exact_quote": "The expected number of windows lost by this construction and the preceding deletions is $o(w)$, where $w$ is the deterministic window count before deletions.         "exact_quote": "$|O_x|/n'<.08$. We call this the *small-cell case*.*\n\n*The ordered lists, $n'$, all training cell sizes, and the table of intersections between the two partitions can be communicated in $O(q)$ bits. From these data the decoder determines $|O_x|/n'$ for every $x$, and determines which samples belong to $O_x$ from their flat memberships.",
        "source_id": "PM-170.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-training",
          "exact_quote": "The construction has $n/4\\le n'\\le n$. If the hyperplane list or plane list is present, its length satisfies $$J_h\\le n/q^2,\\qquad\n J_p\\le n/K_p=q^{1/2}\\exp(-2g_0/15),$$ respectively. If both lists are present, then $J_hJ_p\\le q^{13/15+o(1)}$. If a performed procedure retained a residual, that residual has the corresponding flat cap.*\n\n*If every training cell in both partitions has size less than $.04n'$, then for each query point $x$ define $O_x$ as the union of the following cells: the cell of the earliest listed hyperplane containing $x$, and the cell of the earliest listed plane containing $x$. Omit a cell when no such flat exists. Then $|O_x|/n'<.08$. We call this the *small-cell case*.*\n\n*The ordered lists, $n'$, all training cell sizes, and the table of intersections between the two partitions can be communicated in $O(q)$ bits. From these data the decoder determines $|O_x|/n'$ for every $x$, and determines which samples belong to $O_x$ from their flat memberships.",
          "source_id": "PM-170.1"
        }

For each $x$, let $\\mathcal H_x$ be any family of hyperplanes through $x$ such that $q|X_x\\cap H|/n'\\le2$ for every $H\\in\\mathcal H_x$. For distinct members set $$\\theta_{HH'}=\\frac{q|X_x\\cap H\\cap H'|}{n'},$$ and let $E_x(a)$ count ordered distinct pairs with $\\theta_{HH'}\\ge a$. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Ramsey number** ([source](#sec:introduction)): For integers $s,t\ge2$, the Ramsey number $r(s,t)$ is the least integer $n$ such that every simple graph on $n$ vertices contains either a complete graph $K_s$ or an independent set of size $t$.
- **Independence number** ([source](#sec:introduction)): We write $\alpha(G)$ for the largest size of an independent set in $G$.
- **Projective point spaces** ([source](#pm-h-7)): For a prime power $q$, let $\mathcal P_d=\mathop{\mathrm{PG}}(d,q)$ be the one-dimensional subspaces of $\mathbb F_q^{d+1}$, and let $\mathcal P_d^*$ be its dual point space.
- **Projective flat** ([source](#pm-h-7)): In both spaces, a projective $j$-flat corresponds to a vector subspace of dimension $j+1$.
- **Projective point count** ([source](#pm-h-7)): Put $Q_j=1+q+\cdots+q^j$.
- **Flag** ([source](#pm-h-8)): A *flag* is a pair $(a,b)\in\mathcal P_4\times\mathcal P_4^*$ with $a\perp b$.
- **Ordered flag graph** ([source](#pm-h-8)): Its graph has vertex set $\{1,\ldots,N\}$; writing $X_i=(a_i,b_i)$, its edge rule for $i<j$ is $$i\sim j \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad a_i\perp b_j\ \hbox{ and }\ a_j\not\perp b_i.$$
- **Shannon entropy** ([source](#pm-h-9)): For a finite-valued variable $Y$ with masses $p_y$, write $H(Y)=-\sum_y p_y\log p_y$, with $0\log0=0$.
- **Mutual information and total correlation** ([source](#pm-h-9)): Mutual information and total correlation are $$I(Y;Z)=H(Y)+H(Z)-H(Y,Z),\qquad \mathop{\mathrm{TC}}(Y_1,\ldots,Y_m)=\sum_iH(Y_i)-H(Y_1,\ldots,Y_m).$$
- **Relative entropy** ([source](#pm-h-9)): For probability masses $P,Q$ on a finite set, their relative entropy is $$\mathop{\mathrm{D}}(P\Vert Q)=\sum_xP(x)\log\frac{P(x)}{Q(x)},$$ with value infinity if $P$ charges a zero of $Q$.
- **Dominated stream law** ([source](#pm-h-9)): Say a stream law has *density at most $C_0$* if its probability of every stream event is at most $C_0$ times the iid uniform stream probability.
- **Public table** ([source](#sec:predictor-statement)): For precision, a public table is a sequence of independent proposed sample rows, with its distribution specified by the decoder’s current information.
- **Pencil** ([source](#sec:predictor-score)): A *pencil* at $x$ is the family of all hyperplanes containing $x$.

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R1** [claim](#stmt-thm-upper) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-sample-triangle-free-2): Uniform independent sets, induced sampling, and iteration across clique restrictions. -> The classical upper bound.
- **R2** [claim](#stmt-lem-stream-entropy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-stream-entropy): A union bound over possible positions bounds each extracted-tuple atom. -> The selected-tuple entropy lower bound.
- **R3** [claim](#stmt-thm-predictor) / [proof](#sec:predictor-completion): Dimension reduction, overlap estimates, Poisson scores, and bounded public-table search. -> A describable set capturing hidden training mass.
- **R4** [claim](#stmt-prop-compression-stage) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-compression-stage): Marking, pre-round decoupling, geometric classification, and balanced-tree validation. -> A retained tuple with a context independent of the old decoder context.
- **R5** [claim](#stmt-thm-stream) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-stream): Repeated compression followed by a final marking description contradicts entropy. -> Existence of the clique-free stream with small independence number.
- **R6** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-score-procedure): Prime interpolation and fixed-slack limiting argument. -> The sharp logarithmic exponent along all sufficiently large integers.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **I-incidence**: Projective point-hyperplane incidence has constant row and column sums and the exact Gram identity stated in the incidence proof.
- **I-threshold**: The lower Ramsey bound is eventual, with its threshold allowed to depend on the fixed logarithmic slack.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **A-runtime**: Treat the short sparse-pair message as an efficient search or decoding algorithm. -> A short description does not provide the missing running-time bound.
- **A-small**: Apply the asymptotic lower bound at an arbitrary finite Ramsey parameter. -> The parameter may lie below the unspecified threshold.
- **A-deduplicate**: Merge repeated flags when constructing the ordered graph. -> Merging values changes the vertex set and can change independent-set sizes.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The finite projective incidence calculation is explicitly constructed from one-dimensional subspaces and orthogonality. This kernel checks the incidence identity; it does not implement the asymptotic Ramsey construction or its compression proof.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON object with prime q and projective dimension d.
# PRECONDITIONS: q in {2,3,5}; d in {2,3,4}; q**(d+1) <= 125.
# OUTPUTS: Canonically ordered projective representatives and their incidence matrix.
# TIME: O(q**(2*d)*(d+1)) exact modular operations.
# SPACE: O(q**(2*d)) integers for the incidence matrix.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact Python integers and python-flint fmpz modular dot products.
# FAILURE: Invalid input raises ValueError; no partial matrix is emitted.
import json, sys
from itertools import product
from flint import fmpz

def construct(q, d):
    if type(q) is not int or type(d) is not int or q not in (2,3,5) or d not in (2,3,4) or q**(d+1)>125:
        raise ValueError('outside admitted finite domain')
    representatives = set()
    for vec in product(range(q), repeat=d+1):
        if not any(vec):
            continue
        pivot = next(x for x in vec if x)
        scale = pow(pivot, -1, q)
        representatives.add(tuple((x*scale)%q for x in vec))
    points = sorted(representatives)
    matrix = [[int(sum((fmpz(a)*fmpz(b) for a,b in zip(x,y)), fmpz(0)) % q == 0)
               for y in points] for x in points]
    return {'points': points, 'incidence': matrix}

if __name__ == '__main__':
    data = json.load(sys.stdin)
    print(json.dumps(construct(data['q'], data['d']), separators=(',',':')))
```

    "oracle_code": "import sys, json, itertools\nfrom flint import fmpz_mat\nparams = json.loads(open(sys.argv[1]).read())\np, dimension = params['q'], params['d']\nassert type(p) is int and type(dimension) is int\nassert p in (2,3,5) and dimension in (2,3,4) and p**(dimension+1)<=125\n# Enumerate one-dimensional subspaces as scalar orbits, without normalization.\nunseen = set(itertools.product(range(p), repeat=dimension+1))\nunseen.remove((0,)*(dimension+1))\nclasses = []\nwhile unseen:\n    seed = min(unseen)\n    orbit = {tuple((factor*entry)%p for entry in seed) for factor in range(1,p)}\n    classes.append(min(orbit))\n    unseen.difference_update(orbit)\nclasses.sort()\nreceived = json.loads(open(sys.argv[2]).read())\nassert set(received) == {'points','incidence'}\nassert received['points'] == [list(v) for v in classes]\nsize = sum(p**power for power in range(dimension+1))\nassert len(classes) == size and sys.argv[3] == str(size)\nrows = received['incidence']\nassert len(rows) == size\n# Check every claimed incidence, with integer polynomial evaluation.\nfor row_id, row in enumerate(rows):\n    assert len(row) == size and all(type(bit) is int and bit in (0,1) for bit in row)\n    for col_id, bit in enumerate(row):\n        coefficients = [u*v for u,v in zip(classes[row_id], classes[col_id])]\n        residue = 0\n        for coefficient in coefficients:\n            residue = (residue+coefficient)%p\n        assert bit == (residue == 0)\n# Independently certify the stated exact Gram identity over the integers.\nA = fmpz_mat(rows)\nG = A * A.transpose()\ncommon = sum(p**power for power in range(dimension-1))\ndiagonal = sum(p**power for power in range(dimension))\nfor i in range(size):\n    assert sum(rows[i]) == diagonal\n    for j in range(size):\n        assert G[i,j] == (p**(dimension-1) if i==j else 0)+common\nprint('exact incidence and Gram identity accepted')\n",
    "output_contract": "The complete ordered projective representatives and their binary point-hyperplane incidence matrix.",
    "reason": "The finite projective incidence calculation is explicitly constructed from one-dimensional subspaces and orthogonality. This kernel checks the incidence identity; it does not implement the asymptotic Ramsey construction or its compression proof.",
    "reference_code": "# INPUTS: JSON object with prime q and projective dimension d.\n# PRECONDITIONS: q in {2,3,5}; d in {2,3,4}; q**(d+1) <= 125.\n# OUTPUTS: Canonically ordered projective representatives and their incidence matrix.\n# TIME: O(q**(2*d)*(d+1)) exact modular operations.\n# SPACE: O(q**(2*d)) integers for the incidence matrix.\n# ARITHMETIC: Exact Python integers and python-flint fmpz modular dot products.\n# FAILURE: Invalid input raises ValueError; no partial matrix is emitted.\nimport json, sys\nfrom itertools import product\nfrom flint import fmpz\n\ndef construct(q, d):\n    if type(q) is not int or type(d) is not int or q not in (2,3,5) or d not in (2,3,4) or q**(d+1)>125:\n        raise ValueError('outside admitted finite domain')\n    representatives = set()\n    for vec in product(range(q), repeat=d+1):\n        if not any(vec):\n            continue\n        pivot = next(x for x in vec if x)\n        scale = pow(pivot, -1, q)\n        representatives.add(tuple((x*scale)%q for x in vec))\n    points = sorted(representatives)\n    matrix = [[int(sum((fmpz(a)*fmpz(b) for a,b in zip(x,y)), fmpz(0)) % q == 0)\n               for y in points] for x in points]\n    return {'points': points, 'incidence': matrix}\n\nif __name__ == '__main__':\n    data = json.load(sys.stdin)\n    print(json.dumps(construct(data['q'], data['d']), separators=(',',':')))\n",

A scheduling objective or approximation guarantee is ABSENT. |

| other (finite_projective_geometry) | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | Finite projective geometry is the explicit mathematical setting for the incidence and variance estimates. Applying them requires actual projective points and dual hyperplanes over the specified finite field; the later lower-bound argument requires sufficiently large primes. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L01** (hypothesis): The main lower bound is eventual, with its threshold allowed to depend on the chosen slack. Blocked use: ['Treating the lower bound as a certified threshold for a specified finite input.']
- **L02** (unknown): The sharp logarithmic exponent does not determine a constant-factor asymptotic. Blocked use: ['Reporting an exact leading constant for the Ramsey asymptotic.']
- **L03** (hypothesis): The lower-bound construction fixes its small parameter before taking a sufficiently large prime. Blocked use: ['Applying the construction at an arbitrary small prime or varying the small parameter with the prime without further proof.']
- **L04** (hypothesis): The occupancy estimate requires a finite triangle-free graph and a valid upper bound on its maximum degree. Blocked use: ['Using the logarithmic independence improvement for an unrestricted conflict graph.']
- **L05** (hypothesis): Induced triangle-free sampling requires the adjacent common-neighbor bound and the stated sampling regime. Blocked use: ['Applying the sampling guarantee when its degree or common-neighbor hypotheses have not been checked.']
- **L06** (edge_case): Repeated flag values are allowed, and graph vertices remain distinct stream positions. Blocked use: ['Deduplicating flag values and claiming the same graph order or independence bound.']
- **L07** (hypothesis): The clique-free guarantee is tied to the ordered incidence and reverse-nonincidence edge rule. Blocked use: ['Extending clique avoidance to an arbitrary graph on flags or to an altered edge rule.']
- **L08** (hypothesis): Selected-tuple entropy retains the bounded-density stream marginal even when auxiliary choices depend on the stream. Blocked use: ['Substituting an unrestricted adaptively biased stream law in the entropy lower bound.']
- **L09** (nonconstructive): The sparse-pair description has no asserted bound on search or decoding time. Blocked use: ['Presenting its message-length estimate as a practical running-time or speedup guarantee.']
- **L10** (hypothesis): The sparse-pair description does not promise uniform capture of the hidden subset. Blocked use: ['Treating captured points as uniform independent samples from the original hidden set.']
- **L11** (hypothesis): The accepted validation-row law excludes the success of later tests and descendants. Blocked use: ['Conditioning a fresh test on descendant survival while retaining its asserted conditional-law bound.']
- **L12** (edge_case): Training validation and ambient output-size analysis use different exceptional sets. Blocked use: ['Charging the larger ambient exception to the training validation loss budget.']
- **L13** (hypothesis): The rich-line algebraic argument depends on degree below the prime characteristic. Blocked use: ['Extending that argument to a field or degree regime without checking the characteristic restriction.']
- **L14** (approximation): The fixed-slack lower estimate holds for sufficiently large integers, rather than supplying a universal finite bound. Blocked use: ['Removing the eventual regime from the fixed-slack lower estimate.']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 170 (`lean/docs/170.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [The sharp logarithmic exponent of $r(5,t)$](../../preprints/The-Sharp-Logarithmic-Exponent-of-r-5-t-September-24-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [Sharp Logarithmic Exponents for Fixed Off-Diagonal Ramsey Numbers](../../preprints/Sharp-Logarithmic-Exponents-for-Fixed-Off-Diagonal-Ramsey-Numbers-September-24-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The formalization determines the sharp logarithmic exponent of the off-diagonal Ramsey number $r(5,t)$. It proves
> $r(5,t)=t^4/(\log t)^{3+o(1)}$ as $t\to\infty$.
> For every $\varepsilon>0$, the lower bound $t^4/(\log t)^{3+\varepsilon}$ holds eventually, while the upper bound is $Ct^4/(\log t)^3$ for an absolute $C>0$. The corresponding logarithmic exponent converges to three along all natural values of $t$.
>
> The formalization determines the sharp logarithmic exponent of the off-diagonal Ramsey number for every fixed integer $s\ge6$. It proves
> $r(s,t)=t^{s-1}/(\log t)^{s-2+o(1)}$ as $t\to\infty$.
> More explicitly, for each $\varepsilon>0$ the lower bound $t^{s-1}/(\log t)^{s-2+\varepsilon}$ holds eventually, while the upper bound is $C_s t^{s-1}/(\log t)^{s-2}$. The logarithmic exponent limit is taken along all natural values of $t$.

| declaration | kind |
|---|---|
| `RamseyProperty` | def |
| `ramsey` | def |
| `SharpBounds` | def |
| `SharpExponent` | def |
| `main` | theorem |

Comparator source `RamseyFive.lean` (863 bytes, sha256 `9c1d7199f41464d13e0f86715733990dc44b5b3002064ee2794ac5751a043918`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

namespace SharpRamseyFive

def RamseyProperty (s t n : ℕ) : Prop :=
  ∀ G : SimpleGraph (Fin n),
    (∃ A : Finset (Fin n), G.IsNClique s A) ∨
    (∃ B : Finset (Fin n), G.IsNIndepSet t B)

noncomputable def ramsey (s t : ℕ) : ℕ := sInf {n : ℕ | RamseyProperty s t n}

def SharpBounds : Prop :=
  ∃ C : ℝ, 0 < C ∧ ∀ ε : ℝ, 0 < ε → ∃ t₀ : ℕ, ∀ t : ℕ, t₀ ≤ t →
    (t : ℝ) ^ 4 / Real.rpow (Real.log t) (3 + ε) ≤ (ramsey 5 t : ℝ) ∧
    (ramsey 5 t : ℝ) ≤ C * (t : ℝ) ^ 4 / (Real.log t) ^ 3

def SharpExponent : Prop :=
  Filter.Tendsto
    (fun t : ℕ => (4 * Real.log (t : ℝ) - Real.log (ramsey 5 t : ℝ)) /
      Real.log (Real.log (t : ℝ)))
    Filter.atTop (nhds (3 : ℝ))

theorem main : SharpBounds ∧ SharpExponent := by
  sorry

end SharpRamseyFive

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We determine the sharp logarithmic exponent of the off-diagonal Ramsey number $`r(5,t)`$:

$`\displaystyle r(5,t)=\frac{t^4}{(\log t)^{3+o(1)}} \qquad (t\longrightarrow\infty).`$

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

For integers $s,t\ge2$, the Ramsey number $r(s,t)$ is the least integer $n$ such that every simple graph on $n$ vertices contains either a complete graph $K_s$ or an independent set of size $t$. We determine the logarithmic exponent in the off-diagonal case $s=5$. We write $\alpha(G)$ for the largest size of an independent set in $G$. All logarithms are natural.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *There is an absolute constant $C>0$ such that, for every $\varepsilon>0$, all sufficiently large integers $t$ satisfy $$\frac{t^4}{(\log t)^{3+\varepsilon}}
 \ \le\ r(5,t)\ \le\
 C\frac{t^4}{(\log t)^3}.$$ The threshold for $t$ may depend on $\varepsilon$. Consequently, $$\lim_{t\to\infty}
 \frac{4\log t-\log r(5,t)}{\log\log t}=3.$$*

</div>

The theorem resolves the sharp logarithmic-exponent question for $r(5,t)$. It leaves open a constant-factor asymptotic for $t^4/(\log t)^3$.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### History and contribution

Erdős and Szekeres proved the classical bound $r(s,t)\le\binom{s+t-2}{s-1}$ (Erdős and Szekeres 1935) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[ErdosSzekeres1935](#ref-ErdosSzekeres1935)]. Ajtai, Komlós and Szemerédi improved its fixed-$s$ order to $O(t^{s-1}/(\log t)^{s-2})$ (Ajtai, Komlós, and Szemerédi 1980) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[AKS1980](#ref-AKS1980)]. Li, Rousseau and Zang subsequently obtained leading upper constant $1+o(1)$ for fixed $s$ (Li, Rousseau, and Zang 2001) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[LiRousseauZang2001](#ref-LiRousseauZang2001)]. We include an elementary proof of the upper bound with an absolute constant for $s=5$, using uniform independent sets and random sampling.

The lower-bound problem has followed a different course. Kim proved that $r(3,t)$ has order $t^2/\log t$, matching the classical upper bound in order (Kim 1995) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[Kim1995](#ref-Kim1995)]. For $s=5$, Spencer’s local-lemma method gave a lower bound of order $(t/\log t)^3$ (Spencer 1977) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[Spencer1977](#ref-Spencer1977)]; the analysis of the random clique-free process by Bohman and Keevash improved this to $t^3/(\log t)^{8/3}$, up to a constant factor (Bohman and Keevash 2010, Theorem 1.2) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[BohmanKeevash2010](#ref-BohmanKeevash2010)]. Thus even the power of $t$ in the upper bound remained out of reach by these methods. Finite geometry supplied a different route: Mattheus and Verstraëte proved $r(4,t)\ge c t^3/(\log t)^4$ using a construction based on Hermitian unitals (Mattheus and Verstraëte 2024) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[MattheusVerstraete2024](#ref-MattheusVerstraete2024)].

Bradač’s projective construction gives $$r(s,t)\ge c_s\frac{t^{s-1}}{(\log t)^{2s-4}}
 \qquad(s\ge3,\ t\ge2)$$ (Bradač 2026, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[Bradac2026](#ref-Bradac2026)]. For $s=5$, its logarithmic exponent is $6$. We use the ordered projective-flag graph and adapt the marking argument from Section 2.5 of that paper. The improvement here is in the analysis of long independent sequences, closing the remaining gap from exponent $6$ to exponent $3+o(1)$. Earlier graphs on ordered incidence pairs appear in the work of Codenotti, Pudlák and Resta and of Kostochka, Pudlák and Rödl (Codenotti, Pudlák, and Resta 2000; Kostochka, Pudlák, and Rödl 2010) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[CodenottiPudlakResta2000](#ref-CodenottiPudlakResta2000), [KostochkaPudlakRodl2010](#ref-KostochkaPudlakRodl2010)]. The distinction between steps with few choices and steps which substantially reduce the remaining choices is also central to Alon and Rödl’s independent-set counting method (Alon and Rödl 2005) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[AlonRodl2005](#ref-AlonRodl2005)]. Our compression must account for the extra bias created by choosing an independent sequence from a random stream.

The principal additional ingredient is a description theorem for sparse point–hyperplane pairs. If two large sets have much fewer incidences than the ambient density $1/q$, a short message describes a moderately larger set containing a constant fraction of the smaller set. The message consists of samples selected from public random tables, together with geometric data. A score formed from several independent Poisson samples distinguishes most training points from most ambient points. Polynomial bounds for rich lines and planes control the dependencies in its high moments.

This description theorem is used inside an entropy argument. The consistent sequence selected from a random stream has a possibly highly biased law. We retain that law throughout, using conditional entropy to choose representatives and to pay for every message. A balanced tree of incidence tests successively reduces the sets available to the decoder. This separates the geometric description problem from the question of how to use its output under adaptive conditioning.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### Proof overview

Fix a small $\eta>0$ and a large prime $q$. We construct a $K_5$-free graph on roughly $q^4\log q$ positions whose independent sets have fewer than $q(\log q)^{1+\eta}$ positions. A position receives a random incident pair $(a,b)$, where $a$ is a point of $\mathop{\mathrm{PG}}(4,q)$ and $b$ is a hyperplane containing it. For positions $i<j$, place an edge when $a_i$ lies on $b_j$ but $a_j$ does not lie on $b_i$. A five-clique would give five linearly independent points in one hyperplane.

Suppose every stream contained a long independent sequence, and select one. We call its ordered flag tuple *consistent*. Even after auxiliary choices and conditioning on an event of probability bounded below, each possible selected tuple is unlikely: it must occur at some set of positions in the original stream. This gives an entropy lower bound exceeding $4\log q$ per flag by a term of order $\eta\log\log q$.

Two marking scans give almost every flag a set of at most $Cq^4$ joint possibilities. They also give separate bounds for its two endpoints. Revealing a small number of representative flags lowers the remaining mutual information. Consistency then has a geometric consequence: most nearly independent pairs of endpoint laws have very few incidences, except for collisions inside specific subspace rectangles. A uniform occupancy bound on the original stream controls those collisions.

The sparse-pair description theorem now applies to suitable nearly uniform parts of the endpoint laws. Its output supplies samples for fresh incidence tests. A balanced tree arranges these tests so that the total description length is small. The tests retain a constant fraction of the sequence while reducing the decoder’s available domains. A bounded number of repetitions makes the marking description shorter than the entropy lower bound, a contradiction.

Sections <a href="#sec:upper" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:upper">2</a> and <a href="#sec:flags" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:flags">3</a> establish the upper bound and the flag-stream framework. The sparse-pair theorem is stated in Section <a href="#sec:predictor-statement" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:predictor-statement">4</a>; the following compression argument proves the stream construction from it. Its proof is then given in Sections <a href="#sec:predictor-setup" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:predictor-setup">7</a>–<a href="#sec:predictor-completion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:predictor-completion">10</a>, including the finite-field geometry and the score estimates. Section <a href="#sec:conclusion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:conclusion">11</a> converts the stream parameters to Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>.

<a id="sec:upper"></a>
### The classical upper bound

We first prove the upper bound, independently of the construction used for the lower bound. The estimate is classical (Ajtai, Komlós, and Szemerédi 1980) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[AKS1980](#ref-AKS1980)]. Our proof uses the uniform-independent-set method of Alon (Alon 1996) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[Alon1996](#ref-Alon1996)]; see also Shearer (Shearer 1983) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[Shearer1983](#ref-Shearer1983)] for the triangle-free independence bound and Davies, Jenssen, Perkins, and Roberts (Davies et al. 2018) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[DJPR2018](#ref-DJPR2018)] for sharp occupancy estimates.

<a id="stmt-thm-upper"></a>
<div id="thm:upper" class="theorem">

**Theorem 2**. *There is an absolute constant $C$ such that, for every integer $t\ge2$, $$r(5,t)\le C\frac{t^4}{(\log t)^3}.$$*

</div>

The proof has two ingredients. A triangle-free graph has an independent set larger than the greedy bound by a logarithmic factor. A graph whose adjacent pairs have few common neighbors contains a sufficiently large triangle-free subgraph after random sampling. Applying these facts successively to $K_4$-free and $K_5$-free graphs gives the three powers of $\log t$.

<a id="stmt-lem-occupancy"></a>
<div id="lem:occupancy" class="lemma">

**Lemma 3**. *Let $H$ be a finite triangle-free graph on $n$ vertices, and let $D\ge1$ be a real upper bound on its maximum degree. Then $$\alpha(H)\ge\frac{n\log(D+1)}{8(D+1)}.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-occupancy"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-occupancy</summary>

*Proof.* We may assume $n>0$. Choose an independent set $I$ uniformly from all independent sets of $H$, including the empty set, and put $\mu=\mathbb E|I|/n$. Fix a vertex $x$ and condition on $I$ outside the closed neighborhood $N[x]=N(x)\cup\{x\}$. Let $Z_x$ be the number of neighbors of $x$ having no neighbor in this fixed outside set. These $Z_x$ vertices are pairwise nonadjacent. The possible completions inside $N[x]$ are therefore $\{x\}$ and the $2^{Z_x}$ subsets of those neighbors. It follows that $$\Pr(x\in I\mid I\setminus N[x])
 =\frac1{1+2^{Z_x}}\ge\frac12\,2^{-Z_x},
 \qquad
 \mathbb E(|I\cap N(x)|\mid I\setminus N[x])\ge\frac{Z_x}{4}.$$ The second inequality follows by summing over the subsets; its left side is $Z_x2^{Z_x-1}/(1+2^{Z_x})$, with value zero when $Z_x=0$.

<a id="eq:occupancy-fixed-point"></a>
Now choose $X$ uniformly from the vertices, independently of $I$. Double counting gives $$\mathbb E Z_X
 \le\frac4n\sum_v\deg(v)\Pr(v\in I)\le4D\mu.$$ Jensen’s inequality for the convex function $z\mapsto2^{-z}$ yields $$\label{eq:occupancy-fixed-point}
 \mu\ge\frac12\mathbb E2^{-Z_X}
 \ge\frac12\,2^{-4D\mu}.$$ Write $u=D+1$. If $\mu<\log u/(8u)$, then $4D\mu\log2<\tfrac12\log u$, so <a href="#eq:occupancy-fixed-point" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:occupancy-fixed-point">[eq:occupancy-fixed-point]</a> gives $\mu>1/(2\sqrt u)$. But $\log u\le\sqrt u$ for $u\ge1$, and the assumed inequality gives $\mu<1/(8\sqrt u)$, a contradiction. Finally, $\alpha(H)\ge\mathbb E|I|=n\mu$. ◻

</details>

The degree parameter in Lemma <a href="#lem:occupancy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:occupancy">3</a> need not equal the actual maximum degree. In particular, the lemma also applies to edgeless graphs by taking any $D\ge1$.

<a id="stmt-lem-sample-triangle-free"></a>
<div id="lem:sample-triangle-free" class="lemma">

**Lemma 4**. *Let $G$ be a finite graph on $n$ vertices with maximum degree $d>0$. Suppose every adjacent pair has at most $m$ common neighbors, where $m>0$, $dm\ge1$, and $p=(dm)^{-1/2}$ satisfies $pd\ge1$. Then $G$ has an induced triangle-free subgraph $H$ with $$|V(H)|\ge\frac34pn,
 \qquad \Delta(H)\le12pd.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-sample-triangle-free"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-sample-triangle-free</summary>

*Proof.* Write $e(G)$ and $T(G)$ for the numbers of edges and triangles. Each triangle is counted at its three edges, so $$3T(G)=\sum_{xy\in E(G)}|N(x)\cap N(y)|
 \le me(G)\le\frac{ndm}{2}.$$ Retain each vertex independently with probability $p$, obtaining a set $S$. By linearity of expectation, $$\begin{aligned}
 \mathbb E\left[|S|-T(G[S])-\frac{2e(G[S])}{12pd}\right]
 &\ge pn-\frac{p^3ndm}{6}-\frac{p^2nd}{12pd}\\
 &=\frac34pn.
\end{aligned}$$ Choose a realization at least as large as this expectation. Delete all vertices having degree greater than $12pd$ in the original graph $G[S]$; there are at most $2e(G[S])/(12pd)$ such vertices. Then delete one vertex from each remaining triangle until no triangle remains. The latter procedure uses at most $T(G[S])$ further vertices. The remaining induced graph has the required order and maximum degree. ◻

</details>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-sample-triangle-free-2"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-sample-triangle-free</summary>

<a id="eq:ramsey-three-upper"></a>
*Proof of Theorem <a href="#thm:upper" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:upper">2</a>.* We prove the estimates for sufficiently large $t$; increasing the final constant covers the finitely many smaller values. First, if $G$ is triangle-free and $\alpha(G)<t$, every neighborhood is independent, so its maximum degree is at most $t-1$. Lemma <a href="#lem:occupancy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:occupancy">3</a>, with $D=t-1$, gives $|V(G)|<8t^2/\log t$. Consequently $$\label{eq:ramsey-three-upper}
 r(3,t)\le C_3\frac{t^2}{\log t}$$ for an absolute constant $C_3$.

Next let $s\in\{4,5\}$, and suppose $G$ is $K_s$-free with $\alpha(G)<t$. Put $n=|V(G)|$ and $d=\Delta(G)$. Every neighborhood is $K_{s-1}$-free and has independence number below $t$, whence $d<r(s-1,t)$. The common neighborhood of an edge is $K_{s-2}$-free. Thus an upper bound on every adjacent codegree is $$m=\begin{cases}
 t,&s=4,\\
 r(3,t),&s=5.
 \end{cases}$$ Here the first case uses the fact that this common neighborhood is independent, and the second uses that it is triangle-free.

<a id="eq:ramsey-upper-step"></a>
If $d\le t^{s-5/2}$, greedy selection gives $n<t(d+1)$: repeatedly choose a vertex for an independent set and remove its closed neighborhood, which has at most $d+1$ vertices. Hence $$n\le2t^{s-3/2}
 =o\!\left(\frac{t^{s-1}}{(\log t)^{s-2}}\right).$$ Otherwise set $p=(dm)^{-1/2}$. For $s=4$ we have $pd>t^{1/4}$. For $s=5$, <a href="#eq:ramsey-three-upper" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:ramsey-three-upper">[eq:ramsey-three-upper]</a> gives $$pd=\sqrt{d/m}
 \ge C_3^{-1/2}t^{1/4}\sqrt{\log t}\ge t^{1/5}$$ for all sufficiently large $t$. The hypotheses of Lemma <a href="#lem:sample-triangle-free" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:sample-triangle-free">4</a> therefore hold. Its subgraph $H$ still satisfies $\alpha(H)<t$. Applying Lemma <a href="#lem:occupancy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:occupancy">3</a> with $D=12pd$ gives $$t>\alpha(H)
 \ge\frac{(3/4)pn\log(12pd+1)}{8(12pd+1)}
 \ge c\frac{n\log t}{d}$$ for an absolute $c>0$. We conclude that $$\label{eq:ramsey-upper-step}
 n\le C\frac{td}{\log t}
 \le C\frac{t\,r(s-1,t)}{\log t}$$ in the large-degree case.

Apply this argument first with $s=4$, using <a href="#eq:ramsey-three-upper" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:ramsey-three-upper">[eq:ramsey-three-upper]</a>. Together with the small-degree case it gives $r(4,t)\le C_4t^3/(\log t)^2$. Apply it next with $s=5$, using this established bound for $r(4,t)$ in <a href="#eq:ramsey-upper-step" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:ramsey-upper-step">[eq:ramsey-upper-step]</a>. This gives $r(5,t)\le Ct^4/(\log t)^3$ as required. In each passage from an avoiding graph to a Ramsey number, the additive one is absorbed into the absolute constant. ◻

</details>

<a id="sec:flags"></a>
### Projective flags and selected-stream entropy

We first construct the graph and record the entropy that every long consistent tuple extracted from its random stream must retain. This lower bound will be compared with descriptions produced later.

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
#### Incidence estimates

For a prime power $q$, let $\mathcal P_d=\mathop{\mathrm{PG}}(d,q)$ be the one-dimensional subspaces of $\mathbb F_q^{d+1}$, and let $\mathcal P_d^*$ be its dual point space. A dual point $b$ represents the hyperplane $b^\perp$. We write $a\perp b$ for incidence. In both spaces, a projective $j$-flat corresponds to a vector subspace of dimension $j+1$. Put $Q_j=1+q+\cdots+q^j$.

<a id="stmt-lem-incidence"></a>
<div id="lem:incidence" class="lemma">

**Lemma 5** (Incidence and variance). *Let $d\ge2$, and put $p_d=Q_{d-1}/Q_d=(1-1/Q_d)/q$. For real weights $w_a$ on $\mathcal P_d$, set $W(b)=\sum_{a\perp b}w_a$ and $w=\sum_a w_a$. Then $$\frac1{Q_d}\sum_b W(b)=p_dw,\qquad
 \sum_b\bigl(W(b)-p_dw\bigr)^2
 \le q^{d-1}\sum_a w_a^2.$$ The same assertions hold with the two spaces interchanged. In particular, for $A\subseteq\mathcal P_d$ and $B\subseteq\mathcal P_d^*$, $$\left|e(A,B)-p_d|A||B|\right|
 \le q^{(d-1)/2}\sqrt{|A||B|},
 \qquad
 e(A,B)=|\{(a,b)\in A\times B:a\perp b\}|.$$ If $A,B\ne\varnothing$ and their incidence density is $o(1/q)$, then $|A||B|\le Cq^{d+1}$ for all sufficiently large $q$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-incidence"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-incidence</summary>

*Proof.* Every point lies on $Q_{d-1}$ hyperplanes and every two distinct points lie on $Q_{d-2}$ hyperplanes. Thus the incidence matrix $M_d$ satisfies $$M_dM_d^{\mathsf T}=q^{d-1}I+Q_{d-2}\mathbf1\mathbf1^{\mathsf T}.$$ Both row and column sums equal $Q_{d-1}$. Subtracting the constant part of a weight vector therefore gives the variance estimate. Applying this operator bound to the centered indicator vectors of $A$ and $B$ gives the displayed mixing estimate. Finally, $p_d\ge1/(2q)$, so a density $o(1/q)$ forces $\sqrt{|A||B|}\le Cq^{(d+1)/2}$. ◻

</details>

This projective incidence calculation is the bipartite form of the orthogonality-graph spectral calculation of Alon and Krivelevich (Alon and Krivelevich 1997, sec. 2) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[AlonKrivelevich1997](#ref-AlonKrivelevich1997)]; it also underlies Bradač’s polarity-graph estimates (Bradač 2026, Lemma 2.1) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[Bradac2026](#ref-Bradac2026)]. We shall use both its unweighted and weighted forms.

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
#### The ordered graph

Fix $0<\eta<1/10$. Throughout the lower-bound proof let $q$ be a sufficiently large prime, and put $$\beta=\eta/10^7,\qquad \sigma=\log q,\qquad
 N=\lfloor q^4\sigma\rfloor,\qquad
 k=\lfloor q\sigma^{1+\eta}\rfloor.$$ The small parameter $\beta$ is unrelated to the independence number $\alpha(G)$. Constants may depend on $\eta$; they never depend on $q$. Each $o(1)$ is uniform in the stated parameter ranges. Only a bounded number of stages, depending on $\eta$, will be used.

A *flag* is a pair $(a,b)\in\mathcal P_4\times\mathcal P_4^*$ with $a\perp b$. There are $M=Q_4Q_3$ flags. Choose a stream $X_1,\ldots,X_N$ of independent uniform flags. Its graph has vertex set $\{1,\ldots,N\}$; writing $X_i=(a_i,b_i)$, its edge rule for $i<j$ is $$i\sim j
 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 a_i\perp b_j\ \hbox{ and }\ a_j\not\perp b_i.$$ Repeated flags are permitted: the vertices are stream positions. This is the ordered version of Bradač’s $D^*$ construction (Bradač 2026, sec. 2.5) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[Bradac2026](#ref-Bradac2026)]. Related earlier constructions use ordered edges of a bipartite incidence graph (Codenotti, Pudlák, and Resta 2000; Kostochka, Pudlák, and Rödl 2010) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[CodenottiPudlakResta2000](#ref-CodenottiPudlakResta2000), [KostochkaPudlakRodl2010](#ref-KostochkaPudlakRodl2010)]; the reverse nonincidence in our displayed edge rule is part of the specific construction used here.

<a id="stmt-lem-clique-free"></a>
<div id="lem:clique-free" class="lemma">

**Lemma 6**. *Every stream graph is $K_5$-free.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-clique-free"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-clique-free</summary>

*Proof.* In a clique with increasing positions $i_1<\cdots<i_5$, the hyperplane $b_{i_j}^\perp$ contains $a_{i_1},\ldots,a_{i_j}$ but not $a_{i_{j+1}}$, for $j=1,\ldots,4$. Thus these five points span the full five-dimensional vector space. They are all annihilated by $b_{i_5}$, a contradiction. ◻

</details>

An ordered tuple of flags is *consistent* if $$i<j,\ a_i\perp b_j \quad\Longrightarrow\quad a_j\perp b_i.$$ Independent vertex sets give consistent tuples in stream order. Reversing the tuple and interchanging its two endpoint systems preserves consistency.

<a id="stmt-lem-rectangles"></a>
<div id="lem:rectangles" class="lemma">

**Lemma 7** (Rectangle occupancy). *With probability $1-o(1)$, the following holds simultaneously for every vector subspace $V\subseteq(\mathbb F_q^5)^*$ in the dual space: $$\#\{i:X_i\in\mathcal R(V)\}\le C\sigma,\qquad
 \mathcal R(V)=\{(a,b):b\subseteq V,\ a\subseteq V^\perp\}.$$ The same bound holds for the reverse-swapped stream.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-rectangles"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-rectangles</summary>

*Proof.* For $1\le r=\dim V\le4$, the rectangle contains $Q_{r-1}Q_{4-r}\le4q^3$ flags; for $r=0,5$ it is empty. Its stream count is binomial with mean at most $C_0\sigma$. The number of vector subspaces is at most $6q^{25}$, for example by counting ordered spanning lists. A Chernoff bound and a sufficiently large fixed $C$ give the simultaneous claim. After swapping, $\mathcal R(V)$ becomes $\mathcal R(V^\perp)$, so the same event suffices. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
#### Laws of selected tuples

Entropy always means Shannon entropy with natural logarithms. For a finite-valued variable $Y$ with masses $p_y$, write $H(Y)=-\sum_y p_y\log p_y$, with $0\log0=0$. Conditional entropy $H(Y\mid Z)$ averages this quantity over $Z$; $H_\theta(Y)$ denotes the entropy of the particular conditional law given $Z=\theta$. Mutual information and total correlation are $$I(Y;Z)=H(Y)+H(Z)-H(Y,Z),\qquad
 \mathop{\mathrm{TC}}(Y_1,\ldots,Y_m)=\sum_iH(Y_i)-H(Y_1,\ldots,Y_m).$$ We use their conditional versions with the same convention.

For probability masses $P,Q$ on a finite set, their relative entropy is $$\mathop{\mathrm{D}}(P\Vert Q)=\sum_xP(x)\log\frac{P(x)}{Q(x)},$$ with value infinity if $P$ charges a zero of $Q$. Direct expansion gives $$I(Y;Z)=\mathop{\mathrm{D}}(P_{Y,Z}\Vert P_Y\otimes P_Z).$$ For every real function $f$, Jensen’s inequality gives <a id="eq:entropy-variational"></a>$$\label{eq:entropy-variational}
 \mathop{\mathrm{D}}(P\Vert Q)\ge \mathbb E_P f-\log\mathbb E_Q e^f.$$ Indeed, apply concavity of the logarithm under $P$ to $e^fQ/P$; its expectation is at most $\mathbb E_Q e^f$. In particular relative entropy is nonnegative. If $P(E)=0$, use $f=-s1_E$ and let $s\to\infty$ to obtain $\mathop{\mathrm{D}}(P\Vert Q)\ge-\log(1-Q(E))\ge Q(E)$.

We also use the following elementary concentration estimates. For a binomial or Poisson variable of mean $\mu$, halving or doubling its mean has tail probability at most $e^{-c\mu}$; more generally, its upper tail at $h\ge e\mu$ is at most $(e\mu/h)^h$. For independent centered variables bounded in absolute value by one and with total variance $V$, the two-sided tail of their sum at $z$ is at most $$2\exp\{-c\min(z,z^2/V)\}.$$ These follow from the exponential Markov inequality. For the first two bounds use $\log\mathbb Ee^{t(X-\mu)}\le\mu(e^t-1-t)$. For the last, the Taylor expansion gives $\mathbb Ee^{tX}\le\exp(Ct^2\mathbb EX^2)$ for centered $|X|\le1$ and $|t|\le1$; multiply these bounds and choose $|t|=\min(cz/V,c)$. The zero-variance case is deterministic.

Say a stream law has *density at most $C_0$* if its probability of every stream event is at most $C_0$ times the iid uniform stream probability. Auxiliary random choices can have arbitrary dependence on the stream; only this marginal bound is required.

<a id="stmt-lem-stream-entropy"></a>
<div id="lem:stream-entropy" class="lemma">

**Lemma 8** (Entropy of an extracted tuple). *Suppose the stream law has density at most $C_0$, where $C_0$ is independent of $q$. By any rule, possibly using auxiliary randomness, extract a tuple $F$ of deterministic length $\ell$ in stream order or in reverse-swapped order. If $c_0k\le\ell\le C_1k$ for fixed positive constants, then $$\max_f\mathbb P(F=f)\le
 \frac{2C_0\binom N\ell}{M^\ell},
 \qquad
 H(F)\ge4\sigma\ell+\eta\ell\log\sigma-O(k).$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-stream-entropy"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-stream-entropy</summary>

*Proof.* The event $F=f$ requires $f$, or its reverse-swap, to occur at some $\ell$ positions of the stream. For any specified positions the iid probability is $M^{-\ell}$, even when flag values repeat. A union bound proves the atom bound without any independence assumption on the selected tuple. Entropy is at least minus the logarithm of its largest atom. Since $\binom N\ell\le(eN/\ell)^\ell$, $\log M=7\sigma+O(1/q)$, and $\log\ell=\sigma+(1+\eta)\log\sigma+O(1)$, this gives the result. ◻

</details>

Conditioning on an event of probability at least a fixed positive constant preserves stream domination, with a different constant. Fixing independent public random tables by averaging will be done before such conditioning. A fixed finite number of these operations therefore leaves Lemma <a href="#lem:stream-entropy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:stream-entropy">8</a> applicable.

To prove the lower bound, suppose every stream has a consistent $k$-tuple, select one deterministically, and condition on the event of Lemma <a href="#lem:rectangles" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:rectangles">7</a>. The compression argument starts from this law. Every subsequent retained tuple will have deterministic length $\Theta(k)$, satisfy the same rectangle bound, and have a stream law with bounded density. Its entropy must consequently retain the excess $\eta\ell\log\sigma$.

<a id="sec:predictor-statement"></a>
### Describing a sparse incidence pair

The following theorem supplies the geometric input to compression. An encoder knows two hidden sets. A decoder knows larger sets containing them and a collection of independent public random tables. A message describes a set the decoder can reconstruct. No bound on the time needed to search or decode is asserted.

For precision, a public table is a sequence of independent proposed sample rows, with its distribution specified by the decoder’s current information. Uniform samples in any finite known set may be generated by independent uniforms and a fixed ordering of that set. Distinct calls use independent tables. The encoder sends a row index and any specified additional data; the decoder reproduces that row without knowing either hidden set.

<a id="stmt-thm-predictor"></a>
<div id="thm:predictor" class="theorem">

**Theorem 9** (Sparse-pair description). *Fix $0<\eta<1/10$, set $\beta=\eta/10^7$, and let $q$ be a sufficiently large prime. Write $\sigma=\log q$, and suppose $$\sigma^\beta\le D\le\sigma^{1-\eta/2},\qquad
 L=D\sigma^{8\beta},\qquad
 R\in[\sigma^\beta,2\sigma^\beta+2]\cap2\mathbb N,\qquad P=LR.$$ Let $2\le d\le4$, and let $S\subseteq U_S\subseteq\mathcal P_d$ and $T\subseteq U_T\subseteq\mathcal P_d^*$ be nonempty. Assume $$|S||T|\ge q^{d+1}e^{-b},\qquad
 0\le b\le C_bD\sigma^{6\beta},\qquad
 \frac{e(S,T)}{|S||T|}\le\frac{\tau}{q},\qquad
 0<\tau\le\sigma^{-200\cdot2^{d-2}\beta},$$ where $C_b$ is a fixed constant. Put $d_S=\log(|U_S|/|S|)$ and $d_T=\log(|U_T|/|T|)$. Orient the pair so that $|S|\le|T|$. There is a public-table scheme for which, except with probability $O(e^{-cq})$, the encoder can send a message of length at most $$CqP(d_S+d_T+P)$$ bits describing a set $W\subseteq U_S$ such that $$|W|\le |S|e^{CP},\qquad |W\cap S|\ge c|S|.$$ The constants may depend on $\eta,C_b$, but not on the hidden sets, $D$, or $q$. The decoder uses only $U_S,U_T$, the public parameters and tables, and the message. There is no uniformity assertion for the captured subset $W\cap S$.*

</div>

The orientation requires one bit and may be reversed during the proof. Message lengths are measured in bits, whereas entropies are in natural units; this only changes constants. Integers can be sent with a self-delimiting code of length $O(1+\log(m+1))$. All searches below have explicit finite cutoffs.

We first use Theorem <a href="#thm:predictor" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:predictor">9</a> to obtain a contradiction to the selected-tuple entropy bound. Its independent proof starts in Section <a href="#sec:predictor-setup" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:predictor-setup">7</a>.

<a id="sec:compression-local"></a>
### Preparing a consistent tuple for compression

Our aim is to encode a long consistent tuple using almost $4\log q$ units of information per flag. Lemma <a href="#lem:stream-entropy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:stream-entropy">8</a> says that a tuple selected from the stream requires an additional $\eta\log\log q$ per flag. The present section prepares one compression step: two scans give small joint domains, a short exposure makes representative flags nearly independent of most targets, and the geometry of low-conflict pairs reduces the problem to two endpoint supports.

Throughout this section and the next, fix $0<\eta<1/10$ and put $$\beta=\eta/10^7,\qquad \sigma=\log q,\qquad
 k=\lfloor q\sigma^{1+\eta}\rfloor.$$ All assertions are for sufficiently large primes $q$, with the threshold allowed to depend on $\eta$. A stage starts with a consistent tuple $F=(F_i)_{i=1}^{\ell}$ of deterministic length $\ell=\Theta(k)$, in stream order or in the globally reversed and swapped order. Its underlying stream marginal has density bounded by a constant relative to the iid stream law, and the rectangle-occupancy event of Lemma <a href="#lem:rectangles" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:rectangles">7</a> holds almost surely. Constants may depend on the stage, but the number of stages will depend only on $\eta$.

<a id="eq:stage-input"></a>
<a id="eq:stage-range"></a>
A discrete context $\mathsf C$ specifies, for each tuple position, a decoder-known set of flags containing its actual value. The deterministic parameters $\Lambda,\Delta\ge0$ satisfy $$\label{eq:stage-input}
 H(\mathsf C)\le\Lambda,\qquad
 |\text{each slot domain}|\le Cq^4e^\Delta.$$ Set <a id="eq:stage-parameters"></a>$$\label{eq:stage-parameters}
 D=\sigma^\beta(1+\Lambda/k+\Delta\sigma^{-\eta}),\qquad
 K=D\sigma^{3\beta},\qquad K_*=D\sigma^{6\beta}.$$ For now assume $$\label{eq:stage-range}
 \sigma^\beta\le D\le\sigma^{1-\eta/2}.$$ In particular $K_*=o(\sigma)$ and $K=o(K_*)$. We use the parameters $L,R,P$ of Theorem <a href="#thm:predictor" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:predictor">9</a>, so that $L=D\sigma^{8\beta}$, $R$ is even and belongs to $[\sigma^\beta,2\sigma^\beta+2]$, and $P=LR$. The step will retain a fixed positive fraction of the tuple and give it a new context with controlled entropy and slot-domain sizes. Proposition <a href="#prop:compression-stage" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:compression-stage">18</a> states the quantitative output.

<a id="pm-h-12"></a>
#### Two marking scans

Counting ordered independent sets by steps with few options or a large reduction in the remaining domain goes back to Alon and Rödl (Alon and Rödl 2005, Theorem 2.1 and Lemma 2.2) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[AlonRodl2005](#ref-AlonRodl2005)]. The following scan adapts the marking argument of Bradač (Bradač 2026, Claim 2.13) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[Bradac2026](#ref-Bradac2026)], freezing its state between expensive steps and recording its joint option counts for later entropy estimates.

<a id="stmt-lem-marking"></a>
<div id="lem:marking" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:marking-cost"></a>
<a id="eq:deficit-budget"></a>
**Lemma 10** (Marking). *There is a message $\Omega$, consisting of constant-alphabet masks and $O(q\sigma)$ full flags, with the following properties. Given $(\mathsf C,\Omega)$, every unspecified flag belongs to a known set of at most $C_1q^4$ flags, where $C_1$ is an absolute constant. Moreover, $$\label{eq:marking-cost}
 H(\Omega\mid\mathsf C)
 \le O(k)+(4\sigma+\Delta+O(1))\mathbb Eh,$$ where $h\le Cq\sigma$ is the number of specified flags. Put $\theta_0=(\mathsf C,\Omega)$ and $J_0=\log(C_1q^4)$, and let $F_{\rm cheap}$ be the tuple of unspecified flags. Its expected joint entropy deficit satisfies $$\label{eq:deficit-budget}
 \mathbb E\bigl[(\ell-h)J_0-H_{\theta_0}(F_{\rm cheap})\bigr]
 \le \mathcal B:=C(\Lambda+k+\Delta q\sigma).$$ The same bound holds for any subset of unspecified positions determined by $(\mathsf C,\Omega)$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-marking"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-marking</summary>

*Proof.* Scan forward, updating the state only at flags declared expensive. For a point $y$ in the second point system, let $$V(y)=\operatorname{span}\{b_i:a_i\perp y, i
       \text{ an earlier expensive position}\}.$$ Let $U_j^0=\{y:\dim V(y)\le j\}$ and $Z_j=\{y:\dim V(y)=j\}$, where dimensions are vector dimensions. At an actual extension $(a,b)$, consistency implies $a\perp V(b)$. Thus $r=\dim V(b)\le4$, and there are at most $2q^{4-r}$ choices for $a$ once $b$ and $r$ are known.

Choose $l\le r$ maximizing $|Z_l|$, with a fixed rule for ties. Declare the flag popular-cheap if $$|\{y\in Z_l:b\subseteq V(y)\}|\ge |Z_l|/(16q).$$ Counting point memberships in the $l$-dimensional vector subspaces $V(y)$ shows that there are at most $32q^l$ such $b$. Hence their joint flag count is at most $64q^4$. If this condition fails, declare the flag poor-cheap when $$|\{y\in Z_l:a\perp y\}|\le |Z_l|/(8q).$$ Lemma <a href="#lem:incidence" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:incidence">5</a> bounds the number of these $a$ by $Cq^5/|Z_l|$. The available $b$ belong to $U_r^0$, whose size is at most $5|Z_l|$. Applying incidence mixing to these two sets gives at most $Cq^4$ flags: their product is $O(q^5)$, and both the main term and the mixing error are $O(q^4)$.

Otherwise declare the flag expensive and perform the update. At least $|Z_l|/(8q)$ points of $Z_l$ are hit by $a$, and fewer than $|Z_l|/(16q)$ already have $b\subseteq V(y)$. At least $|Z_l|/(16q)\ge |U_l^0|/(80q)$ therefore leave $U_l^0$. Each of the five nonempty sets $U_l^0$ can undergo only $O(q\sigma)$ such multiplicative reductions. This bounds the number of updates.

Run the same scan after reversing the tuple and swapping the two point systems. Transmit rank/type masks for both scans and every flag expensive in either scan. These data reconstruct both states at every position, and every remaining position passes both cheap scans. The masks cost $O(k)$; the specified flags can be encoded using their old context domains. This proves <a href="#eq:marking-cost" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:marking-cost">[eq:marking-cost]</a>.

The unspecified positions and their number are known from $\theta_0$, and the specified flags have been revealed. Thus $$\mathbb EH_{\theta_0}(F_{\rm cheap})
 =H(F\mid\mathsf C,\Omega)\ge H(F)-H(\mathsf C,\Omega).$$ Using Lemma <a href="#lem:stream-entropy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:stream-entropy">8</a> and <a href="#eq:marking-cost" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:marking-cost">[eq:marking-cost]</a> gives $$\mathbb E\bigl[(\ell-h)J_0-H_{\theta_0}(F_{\rm cheap})\bigr]
 \le C(\Lambda+k+\Delta q\sigma).$$ The $4\sigma\mathbb Eh$ cost cancels the expected cap of the specified positions. The deficit is nonnegative because the remaining domains have size at most $e^{J_0}$. Finally, omitted coordinates contribute entropy at most their cap sum, proving the assertion about subsets. ◻

</details>

<a id="eq:reciprocal-caps"></a>
The masks also give useful separate endpoint bounds. At a slot that is poor-cheap in at least one orientation, fix such an orientation by a priority rule and put $u=\max(\sigma,\min(4\sigma,\log|Z_l|))$. Its endpoint supports have sizes at most $$\label{eq:reciprocal-caps}
 A_{\max}=Ce^{5\sigma-u},\qquad B_{\max}=Ce^u.$$ The clamping uses the trivial ambient bound $Q_4=O(q^4)$ at the two endpoints. If the slot is popular-cheap in both scans, with forward and reversed ranks $r,r'$, its endpoint caps and partner caps are as follows.
